| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     2.0000 | t4          |        0.0000 |       0.0024 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     2.0000 | norm        |        0.0092 |       0.0158 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     1.0000 | t4          |        0.0084 |       0.0206 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     1.0000 | norm        |        0.0429 |       0.0623 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     2.0000 | t4          |        0.0003 |       0.0062 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     2.0000 | norm        |        0.0186 |       0.0305 |

* **Fallback adopted for specification: exact sign test with a fixed design effect.** The positives count under H0 is Beta-binomial(40; a = b = (1/rho_s0 - 1)/2) with pairwise sign correlation rho_s0 (design effect 1 + 39 rho_s0). A node is rejected at local level `a` iff
  P(BetaBin >= positives) <= a. No random numbers. The guarantee is **assumption-dependent, not distribution-free**: it holds when the average pairwise correlation of the sign indicators is at most rho_s0. Positives needed:

|   rho_s0 |   design effect |   positives needed at 0.0125 |   size under the BetaBin null |   positives needed at 0.025 |   size under the BetaBin null  | size if conditions independent (k1 / k2)   |   rank r of 1-0.05/9 interval |
|---------:|----------------:|-----------------------------:|------------------------------:|----------------------------:|-------------------------------:|:-------------------------------------------|------------------------------:|
|   0.0000 |          1.0000 |                           28 |                        0.0083 |                          27 |                         0.0192 | 0.0083 / 0.0192                            |                            11 |
|   0.0100 |          1.3900 |                           29 |                        0.0105 |                          28 |                         0.0213 | 0.0032 / 0.0083                            |                            10 |
|   0.0200 |          1.7800 |                           30 |                        0.0110 |                          29 |                         0.0209 | 0.0011 / 0.0032                            |                             9 |
|   0.0300 |          2.1700 |                           31 |                        0.0106 |                          30 |                         0.0194 | 0.0003 / 0.0011                            |                             8 |
|   0.0500 |          2.9500 |                           33 |                        0.0083 |                          31 |                         0.0249 | 0.0000 / 0.0003                            |                             6 |

and at the other levels in use (secondary Holm family of five at 0.025; graph levels 0.0125, 0.025):

| local level                                |   rho_s0=0.00: positives needed |   rho_s0=0.02: positives needed |   rho_s0=0.03: positives needed |
|:-------------------------------------------|--------------------------------:|--------------------------------:|--------------------------------:|
| 0.005 (Holm 1/5)                           |                              29 |                              32 |                              33 |
| 0.00625 (1/4)                              |                              29 |                              31 |                              32 |
| 0.00833 (1/3)                              |                              28 |                              31 |                              32 |
| 0.0125 (1/2; graph, one chain)             |                              28 |                              30 |                              31 |
| 0.025 (graph, both chains; last Holm step) |                              27 |                              29 |                              30 |

Worst ratio of simulated size to nominal (level 0.0125 / level 0.025), maximum over all nine cells and the P3 contrast and over the three shapes, in the regions of (intra-fold, intra-lab random) correlation:

| region (random effects)       | rho_s0=0.00   | rho_s0=0.01   | rho_s0=0.02   | rho_s0=0.03   | rho_s0=0.05   |
|:------------------------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|
| R1: fold <= 0.05, lab 0       | 1.54 / 1.45 * | 0.71 / 0.77   | 0.30 / 0.35   | 0.13 / 0.15   | 0.02 / 0.06   |
| R2: fold <= 0.10, lab 0       | 2.34 / 2.03 * | 1.24 / 1.17 * | 0.60 / 0.62   | 0.28 / 0.30   | 0.05 / 0.14   |
| R3: fold <= 0.10, lab <= 0.02 | 3.20 / 2.58 * | 1.83 / 1.60 * | 1.03 / 0.92   | 0.52 / 0.52   | 0.10 / 0.26   |
| R4: fold <= 0.10, lab <= 0.05 | 4.37 / 3.35 * | 2.73 / 2.19 * | 1.59 / 1.36 * | 0.88 / 0.79   | 0.22 / 0.44   |
| R5: fold <= 0.20, lab <= 0.05 | 6.02 / 4.25 * | 4.05 / 3.01 * | 2.64 / 2.03 * | 1.66 / 1.32 * | 0.51 / 0.83   |
| R6: fold <= 0.20, lab <= 0.10 | 7.69 / 5.21 * | 5.53 / 3.85 * | 3.77 / 2.77 * | 2.51 / 1.88 * | 0.93 / 1.26 * |

Regions: R1 intra-fold <= 0.05 and no lab effect; R2 <= 0.10 and none; R3 <= 0.10 with intra-lab <= 0.02; R4 <= 0.10 with intra-lab <= 0.05; R5 <= 0.20 with <= 0.05; R6 <= 0.20 with <= 0.10 (the scenarios inside each region are those of the grids in 4.1 and the fine sweeps).
The smallest rho_s0 on the grid that keeps every ratio below 1.2 in R4 is **0.03** (worst 0.88 / 0.79); rho_s0 = 0.02 is enough for R3, i.e. for fold clustering up to 0.10 with fixed lab strata or random lab effects up to 0.02; none of the candidates covers R5 except 0.05. R4 is a judgement (fold 0.10, lab 0.05 is
not measured); the pre-set value rho_s0 and the region are the designer's and the gate auditor's to change, the tables allow it. With fixed lab offsets (4.3) rho_s0 = 0.02 and 0.03 give ratios 0.01-0.51 (conservative) and with other corr(g1,g4) = 0.3 and 0.9, rho_s0 = 0.03 stays at or below 0.92 of nominal.

**Cost.** Under independent conditions the fallback has size 0.0003 / 0.0011 (31 / 30 positives), i.e. 2 % and 4 % of nominal, so it is very conservative if there is no clustering. The loss of power is in section 5.1 and 5.4. The fallback is stricter than the plain test, so the plain-test tier can be reported as a descriptive row and the abstract
follows the fallback tier with no choice.

### 4.5 What is known about the correlation (anchor)

Nothing for Samsung. The only data-based number is the training-side SNL pilot: the intra-chemistry ICC of ln(power law / LightGBM) over 23 conditions in 3 chemistries (LFP 7, NCA 8, NMC 8) is 0.15 (ICC of the sign indicator: -0.02), against a permutation 90 % range of -0.14 to 0.25 without grouping
(`anchor_icc_snl.json`): the data cannot discriminate between 0 and 0.2. It is therefore not used to choose rho_s0. Item (f) provides the same estimate from the Samsung inner-CV training sides.

### 19.5 Consistency check (done for this version)
(1) Every row of the section 2 map names a test, arms in 7.1, a cell, an endpoint (6), a margin (9.2), an error-control rule (9.4, 9.5, 9.11), an outcome row (11) and a display item (14). (2) The states of 11 are determined by the tests of 9.4 and 9.5: P+/P0/P- by P1N, P1S, P1-rev; the extent clauses by P2 and P3; K+/K=/K- by K1, K2N, K2S, K2-rev; M1 by Q-H reverse S at g1; M2 by Q-A10 reverse S at g1. (3) Gate G is tripped by NC-1, PC-1, a test-fold read or an unmet freeze condition only (sections 10, 11, 12 agree). (4) Arms used by each test in 7.1 are the arms costed in 16. (5) Margins in 9.2, 9.3 and 11 agree (ln 1.02, -ln 1.10, 10 % in the sentences). (6) Open mismatches for the lead: section 22; section 20.

## 22. Gaps filled by this file, and conflicts in the sources (for the lead; markers `[GAP-n]` in the text)

1. **[GAP-1] "most" in clause (e1).** The approved clause says "most of the advantage came from the physics law" when P2 and P3 are both S, but no tested quantity defines "most". Wording is untouched (D017). A numeric reading rule would need a new decision row before the freeze (for example, median ln(S_A9/S_A1) at least half of median ln(S_D\*/S_A1) at g1). Not applied.
2. **[GAP-2] Secondary family.** J16 says Holm over five tests with tier words; RT F4 says Holm may not be ordered by a statistic other than the one tested. v4 implements the family as a sequentially rejective graph with nested nodes N_i, S_i that reduces to Holm with one node per test (9.5), and tests reverse S at the slot's final level. The statistician confirms it in item (d).
3. **[GAP-3] Downgrade rule and the states.** J16 lowers "the abstract's tier word". v4 also evaluates the P/K states on the lowered tiers, so the title cannot be stronger than the sentence. The tier of a row is computed at the contrast's final primary level without re-running the graph.
4. **[GAP-4] Rows without a defined tier.** The lab-stratified tier is defined in 9.8 (post-stratified weighted median with a within-stratum bootstrap bound); the lab-and-stage indicators are placed in the per-cell prior of delta_c and the feature set of D\*; the indicator row covers g1 and g4 and, through the per-cell prior, the K chain.
5. **[GAP-5] ISU/SNL.** J16 gives only the ISU fallback "same table". The grid analogue on ISU (W in {0, 2, 4} weeks; 2 weeks is my choice) and the optional descriptive replication are filled here; the lead may strike the replication.
6. **[GAP-6] Tests that leave the hypothesis set.** S2/H5 and X1 are not in J16's families and S2/H5 is second on the drop list: v4 makes S2 descriptive and Q-T replaces X1. The old positive controls PC-2 and PC-3 (V3: any failed positive control trips G) are not in the approved definition of G, so v4 makes them reported checks; the approved table wins.
7. **[GAP-7] Blinding exposures.** V3E 11 leaves the choice to the freeze judge; v4 pre-sets a default (3.3(11)) that the judge may overrule before FROZEN.
8. **[GAP-8] K1 tier S.** J16 says "S reported if reached" without a place; v4 reports it with the qualifier template at the unadjusted p and gives it no effect on any title or abstract sentence.
9. **[GAP-9] Conventions and definitions not in J16:** relative-reduction formula (X = 100 (1 - exp(-median L)); V3 section 1 and T1 write 1 - exp(median L), which has the wrong sign for L defined as ln(comparator/target); the code uses the form of v4); k = 0 definition; storage window min(k, k_sto) for learners; D\* tie rules.
10. **[GAP-10] Q-A10 with two forms.** "The weaker of its two forms sets the tier" is implemented as the lowest tier over the two forms on the scale reverse S < none < N < S (9.5); the reverse-S safety test is then run twice, which is disclosed.
11. **[GAP-11] Subset construction.** J16 says "standardised" without saying how; v4 standardises over the fold's 32 training conditions and adds an operational form of the temperature constraint (4.1).
12. **Conflicts between sources noted, not resolved by edit:** (i) J16 section 5.1 keeps FB-1 to FB-6 and FB-8 to FB-10 while D016 adds conditions beyond V3's 17.2; both are carried. (ii) STATS_RULING section 7 power numbers are superseded by T1; J16 quotes both families of numbers; v4 quotes T1. (iii) P16 5.3 had a TOST for K and fixed sequences for P; J16 replaced them; v4 follows J16. (iv) `debates/D013_build_choices/` and `debates/D015_redteam_v3/` are untracked in git (`git status`); the D015 red-team file that this version cites is not committed. (v) `debates/D016_roles/03_novelty_recheck.md` and `runs/prereg/stats_sim_v4/` did not exist when this version was written.

### 1.3 Arms (at every grid cell unless stated)
| Role | Arm | Change from PREREG_v3 |
|---|---|---|
| Paper's model | **A1** PINN-KAN | Hidden width **h = 1 added** to the grid (KAN [5,1,1]), so at m = 8 the KAN can collapse towards a near-additive map instead of being forced to overfit |
| Physics off, same network | **A9** | Same training cells as A1, storage included (stated in code and checked) |
| Law with a non-KAN map | **AG** = inner-CV best of A4 (additive GAM) and A5 (interaction GAM) | A5 at m = 32 only (its tensor terms cannot be supported by 8 conditions); at m < 32 AG = A4 |
| Residual axis | **A2** hard ODE, same law | unchanged |
| External data-only bar | **D\*** = inner-CV best of {A9, A11 GP-ARD, A12 LightGBM} | A11/A12 gain two tuned options: feature set {raw stresses, the PINN's transformed inputs (A3 abscissae: 1/T, log C-rates, log DoD, SOC_mid, SOC_mid^2) plus t and EFC raw and log}, and storage rows {in, out}. A raw-feature-only LightGBM is reported beside it as a descriptive row (what the features buy) |
| External physics bar | **A10** Stroebl semi-empirical | both "as published" and "with per-cell factor"; the weaker of the two comparisons sets the wording (D015 demand 2) |
| Storage axis | **A13** cycling-only PINN-KAN | g1 and g4 only |
| KAN law | **A15** = closed-form law extracted from a KAN (h in {1, 2}) tuned like A1 | g4 confirmatory; g1 descriptive |
| Matched GAM law | **G15** = the same extraction applied to A4's additive terms | g4 confirmatory; g1 descriptive |
| References | R1, R2, and k = 0 as the window-information reference (F7) | descriptive |

Cut from the confirmatory set: A3 (its abscissae now serve as D\*'s features), A7 (moved to the drop list, g4 descriptive at most),
H3 and the G2 "KAN win" (D015 F11: the generator was strengthened after P4), and H7 except the G0 pipeline check. Positive
control P1 is reduced to the G0 recovery check (nRMSE <= 0.15). The "beat A11/A12 on G0" part goes: the model is being tested on
its own law, so the result is uninformative (F11).

### 1.4 Hypotheses and roles
**PINN role, chain P (primary).**
- **P1, usefulness at scarcity:** at g1, A1 has a lower median condition error than D\*. Tiers N, then S; reverse S is tested
  regardless and reported as a safety finding.
- **P2, attribution to the physics law within the same network:** at g1, A1 against A9, tier S. Tested only if P1 reaches S.
- **P3, data-dependence (the "pays when scarce" signature):** D_c = L_c(g1) - L_c(g4), with L = ln(S_D\*/S_A1), is positive,
  tested by a sign test with margin 0. Tested only if P2 passes.

**KAN role, chain K (primary).**
- **K1, readability at no cost:** at g4, A15 is non-inferior to A1 (tier N at -ln 1.10; S reported if reached).
- **K2, what the KAN adds over an additive law (attributable to the KAN's composition layer):** at g4, A15 against G15, tiers N
  then S. Tested only if K1 passes. Reverse S (G15 better) is tested regardless.

**Qualifiers (secondary family, section 2).** Q-H (A1 against A2) at g1 and g4; Q-A10 (A1 against A10) at g1 and g4; Q-T (A1
against A13) at g1.

**Why this attribution, and why not the alternatives.**
- *Proposal's "physics-informed family, map chosen by inner CV" against data-only learners:* rejected. A gain could come from
  whichever map won, and the paper could not name the component that worked (the opponent's point 1). A1 is now fixed and the
  ladder decomposes the gain. Per condition, ln(S_A9/S_A1) = ln(S_A9/S_AG) + ln(S_AG/S_A1): the first term is the law's share
  with a non-KAN map, the second is the KAN's share inside the law. Both are estimated at every cell with simultaneous intervals.
- *Full 2 x 2 factorial (physics x stress map):* rejected. Without the law there is no stress map, so "data-only with KAN" would be
  a new, made-up model, and it would cost one more neural arm per cell. The nested ladder answers the same attribution question.
- *P1 against A9 only (higher correlation, smaller detectable gain):* rejected as the headline. D015 F5 is right that A9 is a low
  bar. A JES reader asks whether this beats what a practitioner would use, and that is D\*. A9 enters as P2, where it answers the
  attribution question.
- *P1 against A10:* rejected as the PINN's role, because A10 is physics, not data. It is a qualifier (Q-A10), and if A10 wins at
  g1 a title modifier applies (section 4, M2).
- *KAN accuracy race against GAMs (old H1b):* rejected as a primary. P4 found a forecast gap of 0.0-0.1 pp, so it cannot be won.
  It survives as the ladder term ln(S_AG/S_A1), estimated only.
- *KAN pass rules "4 of 5 stable / agreeing":* rejected. The thresholds are not derived, and agreement with a model fitted to the
  same data is not independent (the opponent's point 2). K2 is a held-out prediction test against a matched competitor, which is
  checkable.
- *Misspecification dose-response for the residual (proposal 4b):* rejected. It reads as a straw man and rests on P5 alone. The
  residual's role is Q-H, worded honestly.

### 1.5 Realistic expectation (my reading of the files, not a result)
- K1 is likely to pass: the contrast is between two forms of the same model, with non-inferiority power near 0.96 at
  rho = 0.95 (STATS_RULING 7).
- K2 is likely to come out N or none: P4 found the KAN-GAM gap small.
- Q-H is likely to be none (P5: a 4 % gain).
- Q-A10 at g4 is likely S (D011). At g1 it is unknown and could be reversed, since 9 parameters are what scarce data support.
- P1 is the open risk. No measurement exists for g1. The 80 %-power detectable gain is 17-22 % at rho = 0.75 (STATS_RULING 7;
  different model families).

---

## 2. Statistical plan (outline; the statistician formalises it before the freeze)

**Unit, test and estimand are unchanged (D012):** held-out condition, n = 40; exact one-sided sign test on L_c - m; median
| isu_right |     0.1000 |    0.0500 |     2.0000 | norm        |        0.0112 |       0.0199 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     1.0000 | t4          |        0.0031 |       0.0100 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     1.0000 | norm        |        0.0264 |       0.0418 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     2.0000 | t4          |        0.0000 |       0.0024 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0000 |     2.0000 | norm        |        0.0092 |       0.0158 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     1.0000 | t4          |        0.0084 |       0.0206 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     1.0000 | norm        |        0.0429 |       0.0623 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     2.0000 | t4          |        0.0003 |       0.0062 |
| isu_right |     0.2000 |    0.0500 |     2.0000 | norm        |        0.0186 |       0.0305 |

* **Fallback adopted for specification: exact sign test with a fixed design effect.** The positives count under H0 is Beta-binomial(40; a = b = (1/rho_s0 - 1)/2) with pairwise sign correlation rho_s0 (design effect 1 + 39 rho_s0). A node is rejected at local level `a` iff
  P(BetaBin >= positives) <= a. No random numbers. The guarantee is **assumption-dependent, not distribution-free**: it holds when the average pairwise correlation of the sign indicators is at most rho_s0. Positives needed:

|   rho_s0 |   design effect |   positives needed at 0.0125 |   size under the BetaBin null |   positives needed at 0.025 |   size under the BetaBin null  | size if conditions independent (k1 / k2)   |   rank r of 1-0.05/9 interval |
|---------:|----------------:|-----------------------------:|------------------------------:|----------------------------:|-------------------------------:|:-------------------------------------------|------------------------------:|
|   0.0000 |          1.0000 |                           28 |                        0.0083 |                          27 |                         0.0192 | 0.0083 / 0.0192                            |                            11 |
|   0.0100 |          1.3900 |                           29 |                        0.0105 |                          28 |                         0.0213 | 0.0032 / 0.0083                            |                            10 |
|   0.0200 |          1.7800 |                           30 |                        0.0110 |                          29 |                         0.0209 | 0.0011 / 0.0032                            |                             9 |
|   0.0300 |          2.1700 |                           31 |                        0.0106 |                          30 |                         0.0194 | 0.0003 / 0.0011                            |                             8 |
|   0.0500 |          2.9500 |                           33 |                        0.0083 |                          31 |                         0.0249 | 0.0000 / 0.0003                            |                             6 |

and at the other levels in use (secondary Holm family of five at 0.025; graph levels 0.0125, 0.025):

| local level                                |   rho_s0=0.00: positives needed |   rho_s0=0.02: positives needed |   rho_s0=0.03: positives needed |
|:-------------------------------------------|--------------------------------:|--------------------------------:|--------------------------------:|
| 0.005 (Holm 1/5)                           |                              29 |                              32 |                              33 |
| 0.00625 (1/4)                              |                              29 |                              31 |                              32 |
| 0.00833 (1/3)                              |                              28 |                              31 |                              32 |
| 0.0125 (1/2; graph, one chain)             |                              28 |                              30 |                              31 |
| 0.025 (graph, both chains; last Holm step) |                              27 |                              29 |                              30 |

Worst ratio of simulated size to nominal (level 0.0125 / level 0.025), maximum over all nine cells and the P3 contrast and over the three shapes, in the regions of (intra-fold, intra-lab random) correlation:

| region (random effects)       | rho_s0=0.00   | rho_s0=0.01   | rho_s0=0.02   | rho_s0=0.03   | rho_s0=0.05   |
|:------------------------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|:--------------|
| R1: fold <= 0.05, lab 0       | 1.54 / 1.45 * | 0.71 / 0.77   | 0.30 / 0.35   | 0.13 / 0.15   | 0.02 / 0.06   |
| R2: fold <= 0.10, lab 0       | 2.34 / 2.03 * | 1.24 / 1.17 * | 0.60 / 0.62   | 0.28 / 0.30   | 0.05 / 0.14   |
| R3: fold <= 0.10, lab <= 0.02 | 3.20 / 2.58 * | 1.83 / 1.60 * | 1.03 / 0.92   | 0.52 / 0.52   | 0.10 / 0.26   |
| R4: fold <= 0.10, lab <= 0.05 | 4.37 / 3.35 * | 2.73 / 2.19 * | 1.59 / 1.36 * | 0.88 / 0.79   | 0.22 / 0.44   |
| R5: fold <= 0.20, lab <= 0.05 | 6.02 / 4.25 * | 4.05 / 3.01 * | 2.64 / 2.03 * | 1.66 / 1.32 * | 0.51 / 0.83   |
| R6: fold <= 0.20, lab <= 0.10 | 7.69 / 5.21 * | 5.53 / 3.85 * | 3.77 / 2.77 * | 2.51 / 1.88 * | 0.93 / 1.26 * |

Regions: R1 intra-fold <= 0.05 and no lab effect; R2 <= 0.10 and none; R3 <= 0.10 with intra-lab <= 0.02; R4 <= 0.10 with intra-lab <= 0.05; R5 <= 0.20 with <= 0.05; R6 <= 0.20 with <= 0.10 (the scenarios inside each region are those of the grids in 4.1 and the fine sweeps).
The smallest rho_s0 on the grid that keeps every ratio below 1.2 in R4 is **0.03** (worst 0.88 / 0.79); rho_s0 = 0.02 is enough for R3, i.e. for fold clustering up to 0.10 with fixed lab strata or random lab effects up to 0.02; none of the candidates covers R5 except 0.05. R4 is a judgement (fold 0.10, lab 0.05 is
not measured); the pre-set value rho_s0 and the region are the designer's and the gate auditor's to change, the tables allow it. With fixed lab offsets (4.3) rho_s0 = 0.02 and 0.03 give ratios 0.01-0.51 (conservative) and with other corr(g1,g4) = 0.3 and 0.9, rho_s0 = 0.03 stays at or below 0.92 of nominal.

**Cost.** Under independent conditions the fallback has size 0.0003 / 0.0011 (31 / 30 positives), i.e. 2 % and 4 % of nominal, so it is very conservative if there is no clustering. The loss of power is in section 5.1 and 5.4. The fallback is stricter than the plain test, so the plain-test tier can be reported as a descriptive row and the abstract
follows the fallback tier with no choice.

### 4.5 What is known about the correlation (anchor)

Nothing for Samsung. The only data-based number is the training-side SNL pilot: the intra-chemistry ICC of ln(power law / LightGBM) over 23 conditions in 3 chemistries (LFP 7, NCA 8, NMC 8) is 0.15 (ICC of the sign indicator: -0.02), against a permutation 90 % range of -0.14 to 0.25 without grouping
(`anchor_icc_snl.json`): the data cannot discriminate between 0 and 0.2. It is therefore not used to choose rho_s0. Item (f) provides the same estimate from the Samsung inner-CV training sides.

"PiDDM-style" no longer names an arm on the grid. The confirmatory run of A10 on real data is refused by the code until `semi.A10_FORM["verified"]` is set after [FB-2].
**Comparator selection (no test information).** D\* = the arm of {A9, A11, A12} with the lowest inner-CV condition-mean score on that fold's training conditions at that m; AG = the lower of A4 and A5 the same way. Selection is per (outer fold, m) and never by outer test error. All candidates are also reported against A1 (Table 2, descriptive).

### 7.2 A10 fairness (J11 A1.4; D013 (a), (b))
A10 primary = the published form (Stroebl data paper SI S.1.1, after Muehlbauer et al., J. Energy Storage 48, 103803, doi 10.1016/j.est.2021.103803), population parameters by multi-start least squares on the training conditions, **plus one per-cell multiplicative factor on the predicted total loss fitted to the cell's window points by MAP with the same prior family as A1's delta_c**, and the
same throughput input as every arm. **Scope sentence (printed):** A10's factor multiplies the cell's total loss and A10 has two free time exponents, whereas A1's delta_c scales only the term(s) the cell has and the law has one modulation; the difference favours A10. Sensitivity rows: A10 as published (no per-cell factor), and (l) A1 with delta_c on the total rate.
Equations are taken from the SI and the JES 142, 119552 full text with equation numbers recorded **[FB-2]** (the local conversion of the SI is garbled; the provisional form in `semi.py` is refused in confirmatory runs). A10 gets a parameter-recovery unit test on data generated from its own form.
**Two forms of A10 (D015 demand 2; J16 1.3).** Every A10 contrast is computed for the form "as published" (population parameters only, no per-cell factor) and for the form "with the per-cell factor"; the weaker of the two comparisons for A1 sets the tier and the wording (section 9.5, Q-A10). The scope sentence above still applies to the factor form.

### 7.3 The closed-form KAN law (A15) and its matched GAM law (G15) (J16 section 3)

1. **Frozen symbolic library and extraction rule,** committed with a hash before any outer prediction, identical for A15 and G15. Library per edge: zero (pruned); linear; quadratic; power x^p on a positive physical abscissa; log; exponential; Arrhenius exp(-b/T) on temperature only; hinge max(0, x - c). Each surviving edge is fitted on a fixed grid over the fold's training hull and chosen by BIC. **A per-edge cap on the number of free coefficients is [ARCH: cap]** (set by the method-architect before the freeze; the same for both arms). The free coefficients are then refitted end to end under the same loss.
2. **Matched GAM simplification (G15).** Same library, BIC rule, cap and refit, applied to A4's additive terms. The calendar KAN [1,2] is the same in both arms and is extracted identically, so K2 isolates the cyclic map, where the KAN's composition layer (the part an additive GAM lacks) can matter. Coefficient counts and training-side BIC of both laws are printed beside K2.
3. **External test = prediction of held-out conditions.** K1 and K2 are scored on Test B outer conditions that the extraction never saw.
4. **Unit tests (freeze condition 17.2).** A noiseless synthetic law is recovered; with an untruncated library A15 reproduces A1 to float tolerance; G15 and A15 coincide when h = 1 and the outer edge is linear.
5. If the extraction cannot pass its unit tests by the freeze, chain K is struck before the freeze, its weight of 1/2 goes to chain P (P1 tested at 0.025), the K state is fixed at K= with the K1-fail clause of the table, and the table is otherwise unchanged (J16 section 8).

### 7.4 The external data-only bar D\* (J16 1.3)

D\* is the inner-CV best of {A9, A11, A12}. A11 and A12 gain two tuned options, both chosen by inner CV: the **feature set** {raw stresses; the PINN's transformed inputs (the A3 abscissae: 1/T, log C-rates, log DoD, SOC_mid, SOC_mid^2) plus t and EFC, raw and log} and the **storage rows** {in, out}. A raw-feature-only LightGBM is reported beside it as a descriptive row (what the features buy). Training cells used per learner and cell (`n_train_used`) are reported; a learner is not claimed to see the same inputs as the neural arms. The optional GRU member is described in 7.1.

## 8. Seeds

Final refits seeds 0-4 (initialisation and collocation sampling); tuning seed 1000 + outer fold, TPE sampler seed = outer fold. Condition score = mean over seeds of the per-seed condition RMSE, then mean over the condition's scored cells; the seed SD of the cross-condition mean is shown beside every score (reproducibility statement, not a CI). Seeds are never units; no best-seed or best-fold reporting;
no post-hoc seed addition. Limitation: the tuned configuration comes from one TPE search per outer fold, so search-to-search variation is not in the interval; a second search seed on the primary contrasts is an appendix row if compute allows, not a decision input (SRA5).
**Monte Carlo seed rule (D014; NCA5).** The root integer **20261105** is fixed (a date chosen before any scoring, never changed in response to a p-value). Every Monte Carlo step (BCa intervals, bootstraps of sensitivity rows) uses `np.random.default_rng(np.random.SeedSequence([20261105, zlib.crc32(label.encode())]))` with a fixed label such as `"B|g1|P1|BCa_mean_pp"`;
root, rule and labels are written to `seeds.json` and `contrasts_<test>.json` (`mc_labels`) and the root is part of the config hash (a different root raises `ConfigError`). The stress-permutation stream of NC-2 is `[20261107, 31, fold]` (+1000 for the test side). All pass/fail tests are exact binomial sign tests with no RNG. The earlier placeholder seed of the sign-flip p-value is gone with that test.

## 9. Statistical analysis (D012; J16 section 2; SR sections 2-4; T1)

### 9.1 Unit
**Unit = held-out condition** (Test B 40, Test C 13, S2 19, ISU 63, SNL 23); equal weight per condition (the five centre conditions are not up-weighted). Paired by condition (both arms scored on the same cells and windows, and, across the grid, on the same held-out conditions). Neighbour dependence, fold-shared fits and the nested-subset correlation across grid cells are not modelled in the test (stated); their effect on the size is simulated, [SIM: item a]; per-fold medians and the number of folds with sign agreement are reported.

### 9.2 Margins
*Log scale (primary):* L_c = ln(S_C,c / S_T,c); superiority margin ln(1.02) (tier S), non-inferiority margin -ln(1.10) (tier N). *pp scale (descriptive beside every tier):* delta_S = 0.02 E, delta_N = 0.10 E, E = the comparator's training-side inner-CV condition-mean score at that grid cell, per outer fold, pooled by test-condition count; `runs/prereg/margins.json` (E, deltas, hash, per contrast and cell) is written **after tuning ends and before any final refit is applied to a test condition**. Sensitivity rows: delta_S in {0, 0.05}, delta_N in {0.05, 0.15} (relative). The replicate spread is in no margin or gate.

### 9.3 Primary test (D012; the sign-flip rule is retired)
One-sided **exact sign test** of median(L - m) > 0, i.e. the number of conditions with L_c > m must reach a count k, binomial(n, 1/2), no RNG; conditions with L_c exactly equal to m are dropped and n reduced. Tier S passes m = ln 1.02; tier N passes m = -ln 1.10 but not S; "none" otherwise; "reverse tier S" passes the mirrored test (the comparator is better by the S margin, count of L_c < -ln 1.02). Reported with the exact order-statistic 95 % interval of the median (coverage >= 0.95) and the transformed relative figures of section 1. The Wilcoxon and sign-flip rules are appendix-only.
Positives needed at n = 40 (T1 exact binomial): **28 at level 0.0125 (actual size 0.0083), 27 at level 0.025 (size 0.0192)**, 29 at level 0.00625 (size 0.0032); counts for other levels and for n < 40 (blinding exclusions, 3.3(11)) come from the same exact rule in the statistics step.
**Type-I verification.** The sign test holds its size for any continuous distribution with independent conditions (T1, FB-7 closed). Conditions share folds, labs and, across grid cells, nested training subsets (RT F4). Size of the exact sign test under fold-level and lab-level random effects with the ISU-like skew, including the nested-subset correlation across cells: [SIM: item a]. Rule fixed now: if the simulated size exceeds 1.2 x nominal in any cell of the simulation, the statistician's pre-set fallback (for example a fold-blocked permutation version) replaces the sign test for the affected contrasts, and the gate auditor approves it before any Samsung score: fallback in force or not, and its rule: [SIM: item a].

### 9.4 Primary graph (gated; family-wise error rate 0.025 one-sided)
A Bonferroni-based sequentially rejective graph, valid under any dependence; it replaces "Holm ordered by the N-tier statistic" (RT F4).
- **Nodes and nulls.** Chain P: **P1N** median(L_A1 vs D\*, g1) <= -ln 1.10; **P1S** the same <= ln 1.02; **P2** median(L_A1 vs A9, g1) <= ln 1.02; **P3** median(D_c) <= 0 with D_c = L_c(g1) - L_c(g4), L = ln(S_D\*/S_A1). Chain K: **K1** median(L_A15 vs A1, g4) <= -ln 1.10; **K2N** median(L_A15 vs G15, g4) <= -ln 1.10; **K2S** the same <= ln 1.02.
- **Initial local levels.** 0.0125 on P1N and 0.0125 on K1; 0 elsewhere (weights 1/2 and 1/2 of 0.025).
- **Edges (weight 1).** P1N -> P1S -> P2 -> P3 -> K1 -> K2N -> K2S -> P1N. A chain passes its level along its own path and passes it to the other chain only when its last node is rejected; after each rejection the local levels and edges are updated by the standard sequentially rejective rule (a rejected node's level moves along its outgoing edges to the next node not yet rejected). Implemented as `graph_test` (statistician; unit-tested on hand-worked examples and on the simulation of item (d)): [SIM: item d].
- **Consequences.** At the initial level 0.0125 P1 needs 28 of 40 positive conditions; if chain K completes first, P1 is tested at 0.025 and needs 27 (J16 section 2). K1 is tested at tier N only; its tier S is estimated at the unadjusted exact p and reported with the table's qualifier template, with no consequence for any title or abstract sentence ([GAP-8]).
- **Reverse-S findings** (P1-rev, K2-rev) are tested regardless, one-sided at 0.025 each, outside the graph, and labelled safety findings; together with the forward test this is a directional two-sided test at 0.05. Needed positives at n = 40: 27.
- **Graph check.** FWER of the graph under the dependence between P1, P2 and P3 (same conditions, nested fits), simulated although valid by construction, to guard against implementation errors: [SIM: item d]. Power of P1 at g1 and chain probabilities for P2 and P3: [SIM: item b]; K1 and K2: [SIM: item c].

### 9.5 Secondary family (gated; 0.025 one-sided)
Five tests: **Q-A10 g1, Q-A10 g4, Q-H g1, Q-H g4, Q-T g1**. Implemented as the same kind of graph so that the ordering is by the statistic actually tested (RT F4, [GAP-2]): for each test i a node N_i (null: median L <= -ln 1.10) with initial level 0.025/5 = 0.005 and a node S_i (null: median L <= ln 1.02) with initial level 0; edges N_i -> S_i (weight 1) and S_i -> N_j (weight 1/4 for each j != i); the update rule is that of 9.4. With one node per test this is Holm's procedure at 0.025. A test's tier is S if S_i is rejected, N if only N_i is rejected, none otherwise. **Reverse S** of test i is tested one-sided at the final local level of N_i (the largest level its slot reaches), outside the graph, as a safety finding. **Q-A10** is computed for both forms of A10 (7.2): the tier of the test is the lowest tier across the two forms on the scale reverse S < none < N < S; the reverse-S test is run for each form at the slot's final level and the multiplicity of that safety finding is disclosed. Tier words and sentences follow the template of 11 (section 4.3 there).

### 9.6 Estimated (no tier words, no error control)
- A1 against D\*, A9, AG, A2 and A10 at **all 9 grid cells**, each with a simultaneous 95 % exact order-statistic interval (Bonferroni over the 9 cells of a contrast, 9.7).
- **The ladder per cell.** Per condition, ln(S_A9/S_A1) = ln(S_A9/S_AG) + ln(S_AG/S_A1): the first term is the law's share with a non-KAN map, the second is the KAN's share inside the law. Both terms are estimated at every cell with simultaneous intervals; medians do not add, so the shares are reported as medians of the terms and as the mean log terms with BCa intervals, never as a decomposition of the median.
- **Window-information gain** (k = 2 against k = 0, and k_full against k = 0) per arm.
- The mean paired pp difference with a BCa interval and the worst-condition error for every arm and cell; per-fold medians; Test C (descriptive, D012); NC-2; the KAN-law stability and agreement metrics (11a items 6-7).

### 9.7 Simultaneous intervals
Per contrast and cell the two-sided level is 1 - 0.05/9 (Bonferroni over the nine cells; over fewer cells if k_full = 2). The interval is the narrowest order-statistic pair of the sorted L_c with coverage at least that level; if no pair attains it at the stated n, the range (min, max) is used and labelled. Existence and coverage at n = 40 (and at the reduced n of 3.3(11)): [SIM: item e].

### 9.8 Fixed rules (J16 section 2, as amended; no discretion)
1. **Mean against median.** Whenever the sign of the mean paired pp difference disagrees with the median log ratio of a gated or secondary contrast at g1 or g4, the abstract adds the mean difference with its BCa interval and the worst-condition error (RT F3).
2. **Automatic downgrade.** If any of the following rows lowers the tier of a gated or secondary contrast, the abstract's tier word is lowered to that row's tier, with no discretion (closes RT F2.4): (i) the lab-stratified row; (ii) the lab-and-stage-indicator row on Test B, which includes A10 and D\* (closes the A10 gap of RT F8); (iii) the retain-every-check-up row; (iv) the random-subset row at g1 (3 seeded random nested subsets in place of max-min, for A1 and the D\* candidates; condition scores averaged over the 3). Every other sensitivity row is reported and has no consequence. Operational reading ([GAP-3]): each row recomputes every gated and secondary contrast it covers at the cells g1 and g4 (row (iv) at g1 only), testing each contrast at the final local level it had in the primary analysis (no re-run of the graph) on the scale reverse S < none < N < S (a binary pass/fail for P3, K1 and the P2 clause); the lowest tier over the primary analysis and the four rows is the tier used in the abstract **and in the state definitions of section 11**, so that the title never carries a stronger tier than the abstract sentence. Lab-stratified tier: the lab-post-stratified weighted median of L (stratum = the condition's majority lab, equal weight per stratum member) against the margins, using the one-sided lower bound of a within-stratum bootstrap at the contrast's level (MC seed rule of section 8) ([GAP-4]). Lab-and-stage indicators enter the per-cell prior mean of delta_c for A1, A9, A2, A13, A15 and G15, the per-cell factor prior for A10 and the feature set for D\*.
3. **Test C in the abstract.** Test C appears in the abstract in its fixed D011 sentence (section 11, "Test C wording") only if the lab-indicator row leaves the sign of its median ratio unchanged. Otherwise it goes to Limitations. It is always reported in the Results. This is a rule, not a choice.

### 9.9 Sensitivity rows (appendix; only rows (i)-(iv) of 9.8 have a consequence)
(a) lab-stratified bootstrap and the post-stratified median/mean (**downgrade row**); (b) mean paired difference per lab and per stage, with n; (c) leave-one-lab-out; (d) two-stage bootstrap (conditions, then cells), weights by scored cells; (e) pp-scale row; (f) **lab-and-stage indicator row on Test B at g1 and g4, including A10 and D\* (downgrade row)**, and on Test C (identified only under "no lab x temperature interaction"); (g) *A10 as published is no longer a sensitivity row: both forms are primary (7.2)*; (h) **retain-every-check-up (outlier rule off) (downgrade row)**; (i) AT included; storage cells 04-06 included; cells with >= 3 forecast points; forecast-failure rule 3.3(8); oracle throughput; backbone fixed to self-limiting; windows k_full - 1 and k_full + 2 at g4; the cells excluded under 3.3(11) scored; (j) margin sensitivities of 9.2; (k) optimiser recipe: clip {10, 50} and warm-up {0.2, 0.6} for P2 at g1 and g4; (l) delta scope "total" (delta_c on the total rate, A1); (m) A2-only tournament sensitivity: Y0 {0.5, 2}, gamma {0.15, 0.6}, TAU {0.075, 0.3}; (n) optional appendix row "separate h_cal and h_cyc" for A2 (RK4), descriptive, only if CPU remains after the core set; (o) **random-subset row at g1 (downgrade row)**; (p) a second TPE search seed on P1 and P2 if compute allows (appendix, no decision input).
Per fold (D013 C7): tournament table, tuned lam_res with edge share, sd of fitted delta_c on training cells against TAU (flag outside [0.5, 2] x 0.15), clipped-step fraction for A1 and A9 (`diagnostics.json`). Re-open triggers recorded in D013 stand except "a sensitivity row changes a primary tier", which is superseded by the automatic rule of 9.8 for rows (i)-(iv) and informational for the others.

### 9.10 Reference quantities and effect sizes
**Reference quantities (descriptive, never margins or gates).** `R_LOO` (replicate spread) and `R_WARP` (window-aware replicate predictor, SR 1.2, the form with the lower training-side median per fold) are computed on training sides after parsing; Table 1 reports `E/R_LOO` and `E/R_WARP`. They decide nothing.
**Effect sizes (always shown):** median condition log ratio and relative reduction with the exact interval; mean paired difference in pp with BCa interval (descriptive); worst-condition error; share of conditions won; per-fold medians. Test C and S2 are descriptive; NC-2 is interpretive; exploratory results are labelled.

### 9.11 Alpha accounting (RT F4)
| Test | Level | Where |
|---|---|---|
| P1N, P1S, P2, P3, K1, K2N, K2S | graph, family-wise 0.025 one-sided | 9.4 |
| P1-rev, K2-rev | 0.025 one-sided each, outside the graph (safety findings; with the forward test a directional two-sided 0.05) | 9.4 |
| Q-A10 g1, g4; Q-H g1, g4; Q-T g1 | secondary graph, family-wise 0.025 | 9.5 |
| reverse S of the five qualifiers | the slot's final level, outside the graph (safety findings) | 9.5 |
| NC-2 | 0.025, interpretive, changes no tier | 10 |
| K1 tier S | unadjusted, descriptive | 9.4 |
| Test C, S2, Test A, S3, ladder, window-information gain, all other contrasts | none; intervals only | 9.6 |
Sensitivity rows have no alpha; they act only through 9.8 rule 2.

## 10. Controls (D014; NC sections 3 and 6; J16 1.3)
- **NC-1 (gate G): label invariance.** Purpose: detect any flow from held-out post-window labels into predictions, tuning or selection. Procedure: every held-out cell's post-window retention is replaced by garbage (`ret_new = ret[w] - k_c (ret[w] - ret) + eps`, k_c ~ U(0.3, 2.5) per cell, eps ~ N(0, 0.02^2), clipped to [0, 1.1]; with a life level L: `ret_new = L + k_c (ret - L) + eps`, so the forecast times are unchanged and asserted),
  applied to the underlying cell object so that any copy carries the same garbage; window points and training cells untouched. **Pass iff** (a) every prediction row of the two runs (all arms, all seeds, same cells and times) is identical, max |difference| = 0 (same machine, same commit, one thread; if a platform ever breaks bitwise reproducibility the tolerance is 1e-12 and a clean-vs-clean repeat must show the same), and (b) `tuning_done.json` and
  `margins_inputs.json` are identical. Any difference trips gate G. Run (1) **before the freeze, mandatory [FB-9]:** `mode: nc1_full` on the synthetic G2 design at Samsung size, the complete pipeline, at the candidate frozen commit, all confirmatory arms, one fold, `nc1_full.json` filed in the checklist; (2) **in every confirmatory run**, after the final predictions, each fitted arm and seed re-predicts from
  windows rebuilt out of scrambled cells and writes `invariance.json`; a difference writes `GATE_G_TRIPPED` and raises `InvarianceError`. The guard's train/test disjointness assertions (cell ids and condition keys) stay and are cited as the real-data analogue of the grouping-error leak. Evidence at demo budgets (NCA4): 0 of 9 clean datasets flagged, 9 of 9 flagged for each of two deliberate leaks.
  NC-1 is blind to what does not flow from labels through the code (human selection after looking, covariate use such as the design hull); stop rule 6 covers the former, E4 the latter. No arm reads the extent of its own forecast set (tested for all 15 arms).
  **Grid coverage.** NC-1 runs in every confirmatory run, i.e. for every (outer fold, m) tuning record and every (m, k) prediction set. The mandatory pre-freeze full-pipeline run [FB-9] is made at the candidate frozen commit, which contains the D016 code paths (subset selector, k = 0 and k = 2 windows, A15/G15 extraction), on one fold at m in {8, 32}; the synthetic Samsung-size design serves only as a data container and no G2 inference is drawn from it.
- **NC-2 (interpretive, not gating): do the stress functions carry the gain?** Run A1 (A9 optional) on the Test B folds at g4 with the stress vectors (with design throughput) permuted among conditions, separately in training and test (`permute_stress`, Test B only), and compare with the real g4 run: L_c = ln(S_A1,permuted,c / S_A1,real,c), paired by condition. Criterion: exact one-sided sign test of median(L) > 0 at 0.025
  (27 of 40 positive). Consequence: pass licenses the Discussion sentence "the accuracy of the learned law depends on the stress dependence it learns"; fail removes it and the Discussion says stress dependence was not shown necessary for the accuracy (E1). It does not stop the study and changes no tier. Descriptive rows (no criterion): L(A9/A1) under real and permuted stress, scores of A1, A9, R1, R2 under permutation.
| 10 | NC-1 grid coverage; NC-2 at g4; positive controls: PC-1 (G0, gate) reduced; PC-2, PC-3 become reported control checks | J16 1.3, 4.1 (gate G); NC; D014 |
| 11 | Outcome table replaced by J16 section 4 verbatim; Test C wording and pilot P3 entry retained; reading note for internal references | J16 4; D017; RT F2 |

"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command "Get-Content 'C:\\Users\\Shlok\\Downloads\\PINN KAN battery paper\\debates\\D016_roles\\02_judge.md' | Select-Object -Skip 15 -First 100; Get-Content 'C:\\Users\\Shlok\\Downloads\\PINN KAN battery paper\\runs\\prereg\\stats_sim_v4\\RESULT.md' | Select-Object -Skip 15 -First 34; Get-Content 'C:\\Users\\Shlok\\Downloads\\PINN KAN battery paper\\runs\\prereg\\PREREG_v4.md' | Select-Object -Skip 55 -First 32" in C:\Users\Shlok\Downloads\PINN KAN battery paper\debates\codex_panel
 succeeded in 329ms:
1. **Learning-curve inference replaces the four-step fixed sequence.** One gated threshold claim at the scarcest cell, one gated
   trend claim, and the whole 3 x 3 grid estimated with simultaneous intervals and no tier words.
2. **Attribution through a matched ladder** at every grid cell: the same network without the law (A9), then the law with the best
   GAM stress map, then the law with the KAN (A1), plus the hard ODE (A2) for the residual. A full 2 x 2 factorial is rejected,
   because a "KAN stress map without a law" does not exist in this model class (section 1.4).
3. **The KAN is judged on held-out prediction against a matched, equally simplified GAM law.** The arbitrary pass thresholds
   ("4 of 5 inputs stable / agreeing with A10") are removed. Stability and agreement with the dataset authors' model become reported
   numbers and never decide a title.

The outcome table (section 4) is closed. Every combination maps to one title and one abstract claim sentence through
deterministic rules, with no row left to the lead or to Shlok. Shlok approves or changes the table once, before the freeze.

## 1. Final confirmatory design ("Design R2")

### 1.1 Question
How few cycling ageing tests and early check-ups does a physics-informed neural network with Kolmogorov-Arnold stress functions
need to forecast held-out cycling conditions of the Samsung 50E campaign? How much of any advantage comes from the physics law and
how much from the KAN? Does the KAN yield a closed-form stress law that predicts held-out conditions at least as well as an equally
simplified additive law?

The word "few" applies to **cycling** conditions only. The full storage campaign stays in training in every grid cell, so the titles
say "few cycling tests", never "few ageing tests". The proposal's wording overstated what is being saved: 132 storage cells stay in.

### 1.2 Grid (fixed now)
- **m** (training cycling conditions per outer fold) in {8, 16, 32}. Subsets are **nested**, built from identifiers and labels never
  enter: a greedy max-min order in standardised (T, SOC_mid, DoD, C_ch, C_dch) over the fold's 32 training conditions. The order
  starts at the condition nearest the training centroid and breaks ties by condition id. Constraint: every cycling temperature
  (23, 35, 45 C) has at least 2 conditions at m = 8. The subset at m is the first m of that order. The lab and temperature
  composition of every subset is tabulated.
- **k** (check-ups per held-out cell) in {0, 2, k_full}, where k_full = round(n_med / 3), counted from member listings before any
  label is read (PREREG_v3 section 5, FB-6). If k_full = 2, the k grid is {0, 2} and cells (32, 2) and (32, k_full) are one cell.
  If the schedule go/no-go fails, the D005 fallback applies.
- **Named cells:** g1 = (8, 2) is the scarce headline cell; g4 = (32, k_full) is the full-data cell and equals PREREG_v3 Test B.
- **Held-out conditions:** the unchanged Test B (5 folds of 8, n = 40, `samsung_splits_v2.json`). Only the training side is
  thinned, so every contrast is paired by condition across the grid.
- **Tuning:** inside training conditions only. The same N TPE trials for every tuned arm. Inner folds: 2 for m in {8, 16}, 3 for
  m = 32, the same for every arm. Arms are tuned once per (fold, m), using inner-CV scoring at k = 2 for m < 32 and at k_full for
  m = 32. The k axis costs inference only for the law-based arms and A9. A11 and A12 retrain per k.

### 1.3 Arms (at every grid cell unless stated)
| Role | Arm | Change from PREREG_v3 |
|---|---|---|
| Paper's model | **A1** PINN-KAN | Hidden width **h = 1 added** to the grid (KAN [5,1,1]), so at m = 8 the KAN can collapse towards a near-additive map instead of being forced to overfit |
| Physics off, same network | **A9** | Same training cells as A1, storage included (stated in code and checked) |
| Law with a non-KAN map | **AG** = inner-CV best of A4 (additive GAM) and A5 (interaction GAM) | A5 at m = 32 only (its tensor terms cannot be supported by 8 conditions); at m < 32 AG = A4 |
| Residual axis | **A2** hard ODE, same law | unchanged |
| External data-only bar | **D\*** = inner-CV best of {A9, A11 GP-ARD, A12 LightGBM} | A11/A12 gain two tuned options: feature set {raw stresses, the PINN's transformed inputs (A3 abscissae: 1/T, log C-rates, log DoD, SOC_mid, SOC_mid^2) plus t and EFC raw and log}, and storage rows {in, out}. A raw-feature-only LightGBM is reported beside it as a descriptive row (what the features buy) |
| External physics bar | **A10** Stroebl semi-empirical | both "as published" and "with per-cell factor"; the weaker of the two comparisons sets the wording (D015 demand 2) |
| Storage axis | **A13** cycling-only PINN-KAN | g1 and g4 only |
| KAN law | **A15** = closed-form law extracted from a KAN (h in {1, 2}) tuned like A1 | g4 confirmatory; g1 descriptive |
| Matched GAM law | **G15** = the same extraction applied to A4's additive terms | g4 confirmatory; g1 descriptive |
| References | R1, R2, and k = 0 as the window-information reference (F7) | descriptive |

Cut from the confirmatory set: A3 (its abscissae now serve as D\*'s features), A7 (moved to the drop list, g4 descriptive at most),
H3 and the G2 "KAN win" (D015 F11: the generator was strengthened after P4), and H7 except the G0 pipeline check. Positive
control P1 is reduced to the G0 recovery check (nRMSE <= 0.15). The "beat A11/A12 on G0" part goes: the model is being tested on
its own law, so the result is uninformative (F11).

### 1.4 Hypotheses and roles
**PINN role, chain P (primary).**
- **P1, usefulness at scarcity:** at g1, A1 has a lower median condition error than D\*. Tiers N, then S; reverse S is tested
  regardless and reported as a safety finding.
- **P2, attribution to the physics law within the same network:** at g1, A1 against A9, tier S. Tested only if P1 reaches S.
- **P3, data-dependence (the "pays when scarce" signature):** D_c = L_c(g1) - L_c(g4), with L = ln(S_D\*/S_A1), is positive,
  tested by a sign test with margin 0. Tested only if P2 passes.

**KAN role, chain K (primary).**
- **K1, readability at no cost:** at g4, A15 is non-inferior to A1 (tier N at -ln 1.10; S reported if reached).
- **K2, what the KAN adds over an additive law (attributable to the KAN's composition layer):** at g4, A15 against G15, tiers N
  then S. Tested only if K1 passes. Reverse S (G15 better) is tested regardless.

**Qualifiers (secondary family, section 2).** Q-H (A1 against A2) at g1 and g4; Q-A10 (A1 against A10) at g1 and g4; Q-T (A1
against A13) at g1.

**Why this attribution, and why not the alternatives.**
- *Proposal's "physics-informed family, map chosen by inner CV" against data-only learners:* rejected. A gain could come from
  whichever map won, and the paper could not name the component that worked (the opponent's point 1). A1 is now fixed and the
  ladder decomposes the gain. Per condition, ln(S_A9/S_A1) = ln(S_A9/S_AG) + ln(S_AG/S_A1): the first term is the law's share
  with a non-KAN map, the second is the KAN's share inside the law. Both are estimated at every cell with simultaneous intervals.
- *Full 2 x 2 factorial (physics x stress map):* rejected. Without the law there is no stress map, so "data-only with KAN" would be
  a new, made-up model, and it would cost one more neural arm per cell. The nested ladder answers the same attribution question.
- *P1 against A9 only (higher correlation, smaller detectable gain):* rejected as the headline. D015 F5 is right that A9 is a low
  bar. A JES reader asks whether this beats what a practitioner would use, and that is D\*. A9 enters as P2, where it answers the
  attribution question.
- *P1 against A10:* rejected as the PINN's role, because A10 is physics, not data. It is a qualifier (Q-A10), and if A10 wins at
  g1 a title modifier applies (section 4, M2).
- *KAN accuracy race against GAMs (old H1b):* rejected as a primary. P4 found a forecast gap of 0.0-0.1 pp, so it cannot be won.
  It survives as the ladder term ln(S_AG/S_A1), estimated only.
- *KAN pass rules "4 of 5 stable / agreeing":* rejected. The thresholds are not derived, and agreement with a model fitted to the
  same data is not independent (the opponent's point 2). K2 is a held-out prediction test against a matched competitor, which is
  checkable.
- *Misspecification dose-response for the residual (proposal 4b):* rejected. It reads as a straw man and rests on P5 alone. The
  residual's role is Q-H, worded honestly.

### 1.5 Realistic expectation (my reading of the files, not a result)
- K1 is likely to pass: the contrast is between two forms of the same model, with non-inferiority power near 0.96 at
  rho = 0.95 (STATS_RULING 7).
- K2 is likely to come out N or none: P4 found the KAN-GAM gap small.
| (a) size of the exact sign test under fold/lab shared random effects | **Not passed.** The plain exact sign test exceeds 1.2 x nominal as soon as the intra-fold correlation of the latent log ratio reaches 0.03-0.05, or a random intra-lab correlation reaches 0.015-0.023. A pre-set fallback (sign test with a fixed design effect, rho_s0 = 0.03: 31 of 40 at 0.0125, 30 of 40 at 0.025) stays below 1.2 x nominal in every cell and for P3 up to intra-fold 0.10 with intra-lab 0.05, at a power price (section 4.4) | fallback specified and simulated; **needs gate-auditor approval before any Samsung score** |
| (b) power of P1, chains P2 and P3, P3 itself | tables in section 5; with the fallback the 80 %-power relative reduction at g1 is 0.21 (rho 0.75, ISU-like skew; plain test 0.17) | reported |
| (c) power of K1, K2 | K1 non-inferiority has power 0.96 at equality for rho 0.95 and 0.73 for rho 0.90 (plain test); K2 superiority needs a 9-15 % gain for 80 % power | reported |
| (d) FWER of the graph | implementation check **passed**: independent-condition maximum 0.0204 over 72 configurations (global null 0.0169); with fold/lab effects the plain test breaks the 0.025 bound (0.088) and the fallback rho_s0 = 0.03 restores it (0.016) | passed (with the fallback) |
| (e) order-statistic intervals at 1 - 0.05/9, n = 40 | exist: [L(11), L(30)], coverage 0.9978 (nominal 0.9944); under clustering the coverage falls below nominal; rank 8, [L(8), L(33)], under the fallback | passed; fallback rank given |
| (f) rho estimation | code path `item_f_rho_report` written and smoke-tested on the ISU pilot (not on Samsung) | not run on Samsung, as instructed |

## 1. Graph function (tested)

`code/pinnkan/graph_test.py`: `graph_test(p, weights, G, alpha)` is the Bretz et al. sequentially rejective weight/graph update, valid for any
dependence between valid p-values. `pinnkan_graph()` returns the D016 graph: nodes P1N, P1S, P2, P3, K1, K2N, K2S; weights 1/2 on P1N and K1;
edges P1N->P1S->P2->P3, K1->K2N->K2S (weight 1) and the two hand-over edges P3->K1 and K2S->P1N (weight 1), so that a chain hands its
level to the other chain only when its last node is rejected, and a completed chain's level reaches the first open node of the other chain
(rejected nodes are removed and their edges re-routed). `sign_p`, `sign_crit`, `sign_test_p` and `order_stat_interval` are the exact sign-test and
interval helpers. 12 tests (`test_graph_test.py`) check: the thresholds of PREREG_v3 9.3 (28/28/27 of 40 at 0.025/3, 0.025/2, 0.025; 16/15/15 of 19; 12/11/11 of 13); the
reduction to Holm; the chain gating (P1S fails -> P2, P3 never reached); P chain alone runs at 0.0125 and K1 at 0.02 is not rejected; P3 rejected -> K1 at 0.025;
K chain complete -> P1N at 0.025 (27 of 40); the sum of open levels never exceeds alpha; batch equals single; FWER under independent uniform nulls <= 0.025. Only the new test file was run (12 passed).

## 2. Generating models (explicit)

Condition c = 1..40 (Test B), fold f(c) (5 folds of 8) and lab l(c) (majority lab: HM 17, INT 19, SIE 4; 3 mixed-lab conditions are assigned
to their majority lab; `cond_structure.csv`). For a vector of J log ratios (grid cells in (a); the contrasts L1(g1), L2, L1(g4), LK1, LK2 in (b)-(d)):

    Z_cj = sqrt(1 - s_F - s_L) W_cj + sqrt(s_F) U_f(c),j + sqrt(s_L) V_l(c),j ,     W, U, V ~ N(0, R) independent across c, f, l
    L_cj = med_j + sigma_j (T(Z_cj) - T(0))     (b)-(d)   (copula form: marginal law of L_cj is the same for every s_F, s_L)
    L_cj = med_j + sigma  (s_F^0.5 U + s_L^0.5 V + (1-s_F-s_L)^0.5 T(W))      (a)   (additive form: the skew sits on the condition-specific part;
                                                                                    medians calibrated by Monte Carlo to the null boundary, 10^6 conditions)

* T = identity ("normal") or the standardised ISU shape (power law minus LightGBM, training-side condition errors, smoothed by 0.2 SD jitter;
  skewness +1.00 after smoothing against +1.06 unsmoothed; "isu_left" is the mirror, -1.01). median(L_cj) = med_j exactly.
* s_F, s_L = intra-fold and intra-lab correlation of the latent variable ("ICC"). They are **assumptions, not measurements**: no fold or lab effect on a
  log ratio between two learners has been measured (anchor, section 4.5, is uninformative). Random lab effects (anchor: labs redrawn) are the pessimistic
  reading; "fixed lab offsets" (labs are design strata, not redrawn) is the optimistic one and is also reported.
* Marginal scale: sigma_j = sqrt((s sqrt(2(1-rho_j)))^2 + 0.001), with s = sqrt(ln(1 + 0.45^2)) = 0.429 (CV of condition errors 0.45, STATS_RULING 7) and 0.001 the seed-noise
### 3.1 Samsung INR21700-50E (primary; figshare 25975315)
Counted from `experiments_meta.csv`: 279 cells = 132 storage (19 conditions: 10/23/45 C x SOC; the 23 C/0.8 centre point has 4 test points = 24 cells) + 147 cycling (40 conditions; 23 C: 10 conditions / 33 cells; 35 C: 13 / 39; 45 C: 17 / 75; 35 conditions with 3 cells, five centre points
with 6, 6, 9, 9, 12). Storage at 35 C does not exist; cycling at 35 C does.

### 3.2 Cell identity and stage assignment (J11 C5; OPEN-1 closed)
138 of 141 zip names occur in both stages, so a cell is keyed **`cell_id = <serial>_s<stage>`**; the fetcher is stage-aware (folders `<serial>__f<figshare id>`). **One stage rule, the pipeline's** (`runs/samsung_pipeline/folder_mapping.csv`): (1) internal cell serial in the meta text equals `serial_internal` of the design; else (2) laboratory;
else (3) first test date (stage 1: tests from Sep-Oct 2021 to Aug 2022; stage 2: from Jan-Feb 2023); cross-checked with `m_0`/`U_0` of the metadata where present. Disagreement: the cell is **excluded and listed**. Open cases from the pipeline snapshot: TP_z10_01 and TP_z15_01 (meta internal serial contradicts the design table) are resolved
against the dataset paper's tables, else excluded and listed; the empty folder `TP_z02_01__f47631268` is re-fetched, excluded if still empty; any cell unassignable on the complete download is excluded and listed **[FB-4]**.

### 3.3 Inclusion and exclusion (rules; J11 C6, C8)
1. Capacity per check-up = mean of charge (step 21) and discharge (step 22) capacity by trapezoid integration of the current (pipeline `q_mean`). A check-up failing a critical flag below is dropped, not its cell.
2. Reference Q0,c: ET_T23 capacity if ET members exist for every analysed cell, otherwise the first quality-passed CU for every analysed cell (one reference for all; by availability) **[FB-3]**. The current pipeline output is anchored on the first CU (fade before it is unmeasured). Retention = Q / Q0,c.
3. **Pipeline QC flags (critical; set qc_pass false) as implemented in commit 3415db2:** step 21 or 22 missing; step durations out of range (22: 0.45-1.35 h; 21: 0.9-2.8 h); median current off nominal; sampling gap > 60 s inside the step; NaN current; capacity outside 2.0-5.2 Ah; discharge not from >= 4.0 V to <= 2.55 V; charge not to 4.2 V;
   |q_ch - q_dch| / q_dch > 3 %; cell surface temperature at the start of the discharge more than 5 C from 23 C; setpoint not 23 C; start date missing; start dates decreasing with file number; and the **causal isolated-outlier rule** (V3E 5; review m1): with a, b the two preceding valid check-ups, r = max(q_a - q_b, 0) / (days between them) the latest fall rate
   and dt the days since b, check-up i is flagged when **q_i exceeds max(q_a, q_b) by more than 1 % of q_i (rise)**, or when **q_i is below min(q_a, q_b) - 3 x r x dt - 1 % of q_i (fall beyond a time-scaled allowance of three times the latest fall rate)**. The first check-up is never tested, the second for a rise only, and no later check-up enters, so window
   membership never depends on a post-window label and accelerating fade (knees) is not removed. Information flags only: file > 30 h, voltage below 2 V somewhere, capacity rise > 0.5 %, stub runs.
   **Disclosure:** the thresholds were fixed after 163 check-ups of 18 cycling cells (19 cells in total) had been parsed; the outlier rule was revised twice (neighbour range -> causal preceding range -> time-scaled allowance) after the lead inspected which of those check-ups the earlier forms flagged (a per-check-up allowance had flagged a point that matched its
   replicate). No model was scored. The rule depends on capacity; a **sensitivity row retains every check-up**.
4. **Incident handling** from the paper's "Known incidents" list. Dropped at file level: TP_z25_01_01_ET_T45 (undefined load), TP_z01_03_11_CU (concatenated files), TP_z17_03_13_exCU and TP_z17_03_17_CU (incorrect SOC_max), TP_z17_02_19_exCU and TP_k11_02_03_exCU (manual charge), TP_z12_*_31_AT_T45 (aborted; AT is excluded anyway). Retained: TP_k[08-11] ET files
   (the failed capacity cycle was replaced by a repeated measurement). The code drops these by `(serial, file number, kind)` for both stages because the paper does not name the stage (over-drop possible). The second sentence of the paper's advice ("potentially consecutive files": every later check-up of a cell whose cycling segment shows undefined load or a wrong SOC_max, i.e. TP_z24_01_02,
   TP_z25_01_04, TP_z07_03_16, TP_z20_03_16, TP_z17 from segment 18) is **not yet implemented** (`INCIDENT_CONSECUTIVE_DROPS_IMPLEMENTED = False`) **[FB-4]**; it is implemented before the freeze or struck with a change-log row (17.4). Segments with only missing log data keep their check-ups and use the designed throughput for that interval.
5. Pauses (chamber downtime 15 and 20 days, lab downtime 4 days; stage 2) stay on the real calendar axis.
6. Storage cells 01-03 of a test point (66 cells) form the storage trajectories. Cells 04-06 (entry and final tests only) are excluded from training and scoring in the primary; a sensitivity row adds their two-point records. The final test (AT) is excluded from the forecast set (AT capacity at 23 C may deviate because of preceding 10 C and 45 C tests); AT-included is a sensitivity.
7. **Confound and non-isothermal check-ups.** All 39 cells at 35 C were run by lab INT; 45 C is HM (66 cells) and SIE (9); storage 10 C is all SIE and storage 45 C all HM; at 23 C cycling INT runs 6 of the 10 conditions (SIE 3, HM+INT 1). Temperature is aliased with laboratory (and partly with stage) in Test C and S2. The capacity test is also
   **not isothermal**: the cell surface temperature during the 1C discharge averages 31.6 C (stage 1) and 29.8 C (stage 2) in a 23 C chamber, with lab differences (stage 2: SIE 28.3, INT 29.4, HM 32.9 C), so a lab/stage offset can enter the capacity (pipeline item 7). Handling: Fig. 2, the lab/stage rows of 9.8, the CU surface-temperature offsets by lab tabulated beside Test C. No adjustment in the primary models.
8. No cell is excluded because of its capacity trajectory. Cells whose record ends inside the window are scored in a sensitivity row as forecast failures (retention carried forward from the last observation, same rule for every arm).
9. **Retention blinding until the freeze (J11 C8).** Samsung retention values stay in `data/processed/` (gitignored); nothing under `runs/` or any note shows retention by cell or condition; the window rule uses check-up counts only; no plot of Samsung trajectories is made. The code enforces it: any non-confirmatory Samsung request is forced to `timing` (fit seconds and parameter counts only).
   The full parse after the download is a data step, not a look.
10. **Disclosure sentences (printed in the paper; D013 C9, J11 C8, review m6).** (a) "Capacities of 18 cycling cells (163 check-ups) were parsed and quality-checked before the preregistration was frozen, without scoring any model. ISU and SNL were explored in a scratch pilot before their splits were fixed; their results are therefore secondary and not blind."
    (b) "The optimiser recipe (gradient clip, residual warm-up) was fixed after a divergence on a synthetic design; the SNL interaction pairs were fixed from the training-design structure; neither used Samsung data." (c) "The data-only learners (A11, A12) are trained on the cells that have enough window and forecast points (when a learner uses window features, storage cells with fewer than 2 window points at the window in use are dropped; at k = 0 no window feature exists); the other
    arms use every cell; the number of training cells used per arm is reported (n_train_used) and the learners' inputs are not claimed identical to those of the neural arms." (d) "All computations ran on CPU, float32, one thread, torch 2.11.0+cu130 (CUDA build executed on CPU), numpy 2.4.2, Python 3.14.3; bitwise reproducibility is claimed on the same machine and library versions only."
11. **Pre-freeze exposures (BL; V3E item 11; default rule, [GAP-7]).** `runs/prereg/BLINDING_LOG.md` lists every exposure of Samsung capacity values before the freeze. Two kinds are distinguished. (a) *Pipeline parse and QC* (163 check-ups of 19 cells parsed and quality-checked without scoring any model; the QC thresholds were set after it): disclosed (3.3(10)(a)), no exclusion; the retain-every-check-up row (9.9) covers the rule it fixed. (b) *Values shown on a terminal during development* (TP_k03_01 and TP_z18_01, one check-up each, stage 2; TP_z02_01_s2 and TP_z02_02_s2, five check-ups each; TP_z24_01_s1, ten rows): none of them informed a model or a tuning decision (BL "used for any decision" column). **Rule, fixed before any score:** a cell of kind (b) that is an outer-test cell of a confirmatory test (Test B, Test C, S2) is excluded from the scoring of that test for every arm and every grid cell; it stays in training wherever it is a training cell (training labels are visible to fitting anyway). A condition keeps its equal weight with its remaining scored cells; a condition left with no scored cell is dropped for every arm and n is reduced, with every positive-count threshold of section 9.3 recomputed from n by the same exact binomial rule. The split membership of the kind-(b) cells is looked up from the split files (identifiers only) and written to `runs/prereg/blinding_exclusions.json` before FROZEN; at present TP_z24_01_s1 is known to be an outer-test cell (Test B fold 1; Test A fold 0, BL). A sensitivity row scores the excluded cells (9.9). The freeze judge may replace this default only before FROZEN is written, in a decision row. (c) Disclosure sentence (printed in the paper): "A few capacity values of five cells were displayed during pipeline development, before the analysis plan was frozen, and informed no model or tuning decision; those of them that belong to test conditions were excluded from scoring."

C:\Users\Shlok\Downloads\PINN KAN battery paper\runs\prereg\PREREG_v4.md:113:- **Nested subsets, from identifiers only (labels never enter).** For each outer fold, a greedy max-min order over the fold's 32 training conditions in the five stress coordinates (T, SOC_mid, DoD, C_ch, C_dch) standardised by their mean and SD over those 32 conditions ([GAP-11]); Euclidean distance; the order starts at the condition nearest the training centroid and every later condition maximises its minimum distance to those already chosen; every tie is broken by condition id. **Constraint:** at m = 8 each cycling temperature (23, 35, 45 C) has at least 2 conditions; operationally, whenever the number of picks left within the first eight equals the total temperature deficit, the candidates are restricted to the deficient temperatures. The subset at m is the first m conditions of the order (so the subsets are nested). The lab and temperature composition of every subset is tabulated (Table 2 appendix) and the subset ids are written to `subsets.json` per fold with a hash that is part of the config hash.

Brackets are filled from `runs/` by the statistics step; X, Y, Z, W follow the conventions of section 1 (X, Z, W are relative error reductions 100 (1 - exp(-median L)) in the direction stated by the sentence; Y is 100 (exp(median L) - 1)). Pilots are always written "pilot P0 ... P6" in full, so that they cannot be confused with the hypotheses P1-P3. The states are evaluated on the downgraded tiers of 9.8 rule 2. The clause (e1) says "most of the advantage" without a tested quantity behind "most" ([GAP-1]); the wording is not touched. The words "weaker of its two forms" (Q-A10) are implemented in 9.5.
